www.lhratings.com  研究报告 1

内需亟待破局——10 月经济数据点评 
联合资信  主权部 
2026 年 10 月，宏观经济延续“外强内弱、新强旧弱”格局，出口韧性超预期构成核心支
撑，但内需偏弱矛盾进一步显性化。外贸端，10 月出口同比增长 35.2%，机电产品占比达 67.4%，
集成电路、自动数据处理设备等 AI 产业链产品高速增长，但集成电路等品类呈现“量减价升”，
价格因素对名义增速贡献显著上升；进口同比增长 41.6%，同样受 AI 产业链与能源价格推动。
投资端压力加剧，1—10 月固定资产投资同比下降 8.1%，房地产开发投资下降 21.6%，基建与
制造业投资同步走弱，仅知识产权产品投资（+9.2%）及高技术产业投资（+4.0%）维持韧性。
消费复苏动能衰减，10 月社零同比仅增 0.4%，服务零售额增速（4.9%）显著快于商品零售
（0.9%），汽车类消费构成主要拖累。价格端，CPI 同比上涨 0.7%、PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，剪刀
差扩大至 5 个百分点，反映输入性成本压力与内生需求偏弱的并存。总体看，新质生产力相关
领域仍是结构性亮点，但体量尚不足以完全对冲传统板块下行压力，内需修复仍需政策进一步
发力。

### Page 2

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 1
2026 年 10 月宏观经济运行延续了“外强内弱、新强旧弱”的核心格局，但内需
偏弱的矛盾进一步显性化，且价格端与需求端的分化呈现出新的特征。出口超预期韧
性仍是最大支撑，工业新动能持续高增，然而固定资产投资降幅加深、消费复苏动能
衰减、CPI 与 PPI 剪刀差走阔，共同指向经济修复的内生动力依然不足，政策托底的
必要性进一步上升。
一、进出口表现依旧强劲，价格因素贡献上升
2026 年 10 月出口表现强劲，增速虽较 9 月历史高位略有回落，但仍远超市场预
期，出口绝对额创历史同期新高。以美元计价，10 月出口 4123.3 亿美元，同比增长
35.2%，较上月微降 1.1 个百分点，已连续七个月保持两位数增长。2026 年 10 月进口
表现同样强劲，增速甚至超过出口，10 月进口 3070.5 亿美元，同比增长 41.6%，较 9
月（31.0%）大幅提升 10.6 个百分点。尽管出口额增速低于进口额，但出口数量增速
仍高于进口数量增速，价格因素对进口名义高增的贡献比出口更为突出，实际贸易顺

差增长好于名义表现。
AI 产业链是核心引擎，但价格因素贡献上升。出口高增的首要支撑来自全球 AI
相关产业的高景气。机电产品 10 月出口 2779.82 亿美元，占出口总额比重约 67.4%，
同比增长 46.1%，增速较上月进一步上行。其中，集成电路出口同比飙升 137.1%，自
动数据处理设备及其零部件同比增长 114.8%，两者是机电品类中增速最快的分项。
高新技术产品整体出口增长 66.6%，同样维持高位。
但值得注意的是，部分品类的高增更多由价格因素而非数量扩张驱动。以集成电
路为例，10 月其出口数量同比由正转负（从 0.9%降至-2.5%），而出口价格同比从 133.8%
进一步上行至 143.2%。这意味着 AI 相关商品的价格上涨对名义出口增速形成了显著
推升，实际出口量的增长力度可能弱于表观读数。
与此同时，传统劳动密集型产品出口增速明显回落。家具玩具、服装箱包等品类
10 月增速较上月走弱，出口增长的动力重新向高端制造集中，结构扩散效应有所减
弱。
AI 相关产品是进口增长的第一引擎。10 月自动数据处理设备及其零部件进口同

比增长 261%，集成电路进口增长 78.3%，两者合计拉动进口增长约 21.7 个百分点，
贡献了超过一半的进口同比增量。这与出口端集成电路、自动数据处理设备的高增形
成呼应，反映出全球 AI 产业链的贸易流在中国进出口两端同时放量。能源类商品同
样呈现“量减价升”特征。10 月原油进口金额同比增长约 41%，但进口数量同比下
降 13.3%；煤炭进口金额增长 60%，数量同比下降 5.5%。国际油价中枢抬升直接推
高了进口支出，而国内实物需求的恢复力度从这个数据看并不强劲。

### Page 3

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 2 
东盟稳居第一，对欧创新高，对美受低基数支撑。分贸易伙伴看，东盟仍是第一
大出口市场，10 月对东盟出口 779.75 亿美元，同比增长 46.3%，虽较 9 月的 52.3%
小幅放缓，但仍显著高于整体增速，占出口总额的 18.9%。基建需求、补库周期及产
业链转移带动的中间品贸易是核心拉动力。东盟仍是最大进口来源地，10 月自东盟
进口 461.0 亿美元，1—10 月累计增长 28.1%。自越南进口增长尤为突出（当月同比
约 55%），反映产业链转移背景下中间品贸易的深化。自美国进口当月约 137.1 亿美
元，累计增长 7.8%，增速相对温和。

二、固定资产投资格局深化，新动能是稳定增长项 
2026 年 1—10 月全国固定资产投资降幅进一步扩大，是拖累内需的主要因素。
国家统计局数据显示，1—10 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅
较前三季度（-7.2%）继续加深，且显著弱于市场预期（预期为-7%）。从当月同比看，
10 月当月固定资产投资下降 16.76%，显示投资动能仍在收缩。 
三大领域投资全面走弱格局进一步深化，新动能成为固定资产投资中稳定增长
项。房地产开发投资是最大拖累项，1—10 月同比下降 21.6%，降幅较前三季度进一
步扩大。新建商品房销售面积同比下降 13.7%，销售额下降 14.3%，销售端未见企稳
迹象。基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，同样较前三季度的-4.2%走弱。尽管信息传输业
投资增长 32.0%、航空运输业增长 12.5%，但水利、公共设施管理等传统基建板块降
幅较大（水利管理业-16.7%，公共设施管理业-14.1%），对整体基建形成拖累。制造业
投资同比下降 2.9%，较前三季度（-2.1%）降幅扩大。 
分行业看，铁路船舶航空航天等运输设备制造业投资增长 14.1%，是少数亮点；
但汽车制造业（-7.9%）、专用设备制造业（-9.0%）、医药制造业（-9.2%）等板块收缩
明显，反映传统制造领域扩产意愿偏弱。在整体投资下行背景下，部分新兴领域投资

保持增长。知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，是投资中少有的稳定增长项。高技术
产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信息服务业增长 20.5%、航空航天器及设备制造业增长
10.2%、电子及通信设备制造业增长 6.6%。设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%，反映
大规模设备更新政策仍在发挥一定效果。 
分地区看，东北地区投资下降 25.9%，中部（-12.9%）、东部（-11.5%）、西部（-
9.2%）均呈两位数或接近两位数的降幅，区域分化中东北压力尤为突出。民间投资同
比下降 11.4%，降幅大于整体投资，扣除房地产后仍下降 7.8%，显示民营企业投资信
心依然不足。 
总体来看，10 月固定资产投资延续并加剧了此前的下行趋势，房地产深度调整、
基建实物工作量形成偏慢、制造业扩产意愿不足压力叠加。然而，新质生产力相关投
资持续发力，一定程度对冲传统投资下行压力，成为固定资产投资中唯一的稳定增长
项。

三、消费内部分化显著，服务消费强、商品消费偏弱 
2026 年 10 月消费数据表现疲弱，增速进一步回落。国家统计局数据显示，1—
10 月社会消费品零售总额累计同比增长 1.0%。10 月，社会消费品零售总额 46463 亿
元，同比仅增长 0.4%，较 9 月回落 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。 
服务消费仍强于商品，汽车是主要拖累项。2026 年 1—10 月服务零售额同比增
长 4.9%，比商品零售额增速（0.9%）快 4 个百分点。旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲等服
务品类增速明显快于整体服务零售。10 月，商品零售同比仅增 0.3%，餐饮收入增长
0.7%，增速回落主要受中秋错月（去年中秋在 10 月、今年在 9 月，部分消费提前释
放）以及部分耐用消费品销售放缓影响。分行业看，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比
增长 2.5%，而整体社零增速仅 0.4%，汽车类消费对整体消费形成了显著拖累。不过，

限额以上单位通讯器材类、文化办公用品类 10 月同比分别增长 6.5%和 4.1%，部分
升级类商品保持较快增长。从前 10 个月累计看，通讯器材类增长 16.5%，可穿戴智
能设备零售额增速超过 70%。 
总体来看，10 月消费延续了服务消费仍强于商品的分化格局，汽车拖累显著，政
策托底品类（通讯器材等）尚有支撑，但整体消费动能较前三季度进一步走弱，居民
预防性储蓄倾向和消费信心修复偏慢的问题未见明显改善。

四、上游价格高企、下游需求疲弱 
10 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月回落 0.2 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，
涨幅较上月扩大 0.7 个百分点。PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差扩大至 5 个百分点。 
从 CPI 端看，食品价格同比下降 2.9%是主要拖累，鲜菜、鸡蛋等价格大幅下跌
抵消了汽油价格上涨的影响。但更值得关注的是核心 CPI 同比仅上涨 0.8%，涨幅同
样回落 0.2 个百分点，表明剔除食品和能源后的内生性价格压力依然偏弱。从 PPI 端
看，涨幅扩大主要集中在上游能源相关行业，汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%影响 CPI 同比
上涨约 0.63 个百分点，地缘政治驱动的输入性成本压力是 PPI 上行的主因，而非国
内工业品需求的实质性改善。中下游企业面临原材料成本上升而终端售价难以同步传
导的困境，利润空间受到挤压，这在一定程度上解释了制造业投资意愿偏弱和传统工
业板块收缩的持续性。 
总体来看，10 月出口的韧性虽然超预期，但其对经济增长的净拉动在价格因素
和贸易条件变化下可能被高估，投资和消费的双重疲弱使内需成为稳增长的主要短板，
而政策工具的传导时滞意味着短期内的企稳仍需耐心。新质生产力的强劲扩张是当前
经济中最确定的结构性亮点，但其体量尚不足以完全对冲传统板块的下行压力。在外
部地缘风险与贸易博弈持续扰动的环境下，以自身发展的确定性对冲外部不确定性，
需要政策在扩大内需上展现出更强的力度和更精准的指向。

Document 023: 2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった 2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から 35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券 35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった 2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは 5年に 1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば 5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは 2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を 60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第 2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が 67％を占めていた（資料 1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、「安全運用が基本」とした（注 1）。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注 3）。 GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を 1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は 0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料 2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである 4資産を 25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券 34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ 8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注 11）。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に 3％台に達し、約 30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券 60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、「国内債券の利回りが 3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注 12）。  
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注 13）。 2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
供給制約への対応力が投資の持続性を左右

○ アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。その背景には、AI・クラウド需要の拡大や各国・
地域の投資誘致策、クラウド事業者の戦略、建設コストの低さなどが挙げられる 
○ データセンター投資が増加することは、総固定資本形成の増加や、半導体・サーバーなどの輸出
拡大、雇用創出、技術・知識の移転につながる可能性がある 
○ 今後も需要拡大が見込まれる一方、立地地域が抱える電力・水などのインフラ負荷は大きい。 
投資の経済効果と負荷とのバランスを踏まえた政策対応が、投資拡大の持続性を左右するだろう 
１．アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。図表1は、同地域におけるデータセンター関連の対内
直接投資計画額を示したものである。データセンター投資は2024年頃から大きく増加し、2026年上半
期も高水準を維持している。主要な投資先であるASEAN向け投資が一段と拡大したほか、2025年以降は
インド、オーストラリアでも投資が急増している。 
また、アジアのFDI流入におけるデータセンターの存在感も高まっている。同地域の対内直接投資計
画額全体に占めるデータセンターの割合は、2025年に約30％まで上昇した（図表2）。

２．アジアにおいてデータセンター投資がなぜ増加しているのか 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している背景には、投資受入国におけるデジタル関連需要の増
加や、それを受けた投資誘致の活発化、また投資側企業にとってコスト面などから当該地域の相対的
な優位性が増していることが挙げられる。 
実際、投資受入国ではデジタルサービスに対する需要が拡大している。Gelvanovska-Garcia et al.
（2024）によれば、東アジア・太平洋地域では、インターネットの普及や経済のデジタル化を背景に、
製造業や医療など幅広い分野でクラウドサービスの導入が進んでいると指摘している。こうしたサー
ビスの利用拡大は、データの処理・保存を担うデータセンターの需要を高めることにつながる。 
また、各国政府がデータセンター誘致を活発化させていることも、アジアにおけるデータセンター
投資を後押ししている。図表3は、各国のデータセンター関連の優遇策を示したものだが、各国で誘致
を目的とした優遇政策がとられている。タイやインドでは、一定の条件を満たすデータセンター事業
や関連事業者に対して、所得税等の免税措置にまで踏み込んでいる。 
対して、投資側企業にとっては、需要地の近くにデータセンターを整備することによる、自社サー
ビスの品質向上が期待される1。事実、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件の企業発表にお
いても、アジアへの投資目的として、現地企業や政府への低遅延サービス提供、通信環境の強靭化な
どが挙げられている2。 
サービス品質向上に加え、ASEANやインド等における建設コストの低さも投資を後押しする要因とな
っている。図表4は、データセンターにおけるIT負荷容量1メガワットあたりの建設コストを比較した
ものだが、インドやベトナム、インドネシアなどはデータセンターの建設費用が相対的に小さく、シ
ンガポール等の地域に比べ、低コストでデータセンターの容量増強が可能である。

３．データセンター投資はアジア経済に何をもたらすのか 
データセンター投資の拡大は、アジア経済にどのような影響をもたらすのだろうか。主なポイント
は以下の4つである。 
まず第一に、総固定資本形成の押し上げである。データセンターの設置時、企業は建物を建設する
ほか、サーバーや受変電、冷却、通信等の設備を購入する。こうした企業活動は、GDP統計上では総固
定資本形成中の建設・機械設備投資として計上され、経済成長率の押し上げ要因となる3。実際、オー
ストラリアでは2025年以降、情報・通信部門の機械投資がGDP成長率を押し上げており、データセンタ
ー投資拡大の影響がうかがえる。 
また第二のポイントは、AI関連財の輸出拡大である。東アジアやASEANは、半導体やサーバー等の製
造拠点であり、製品需要の拡大は輸出の追い風となる。図表5に示す通り、アジアのAI関連財輸出はAI
ブームのもとで急拡大が続いている。とりわけ、2026年以降は中国などアジア域内向け輸出の増勢が
顕著であり、アジア域内におけるデータセンター設置拡大の影響がみてとれる。 
そして第三のポイントは、雇用の拡大である。データセンターの設置時に建設や設備設置の作業員
が必要となるほか、稼働後も設備管理やサーバー運用に関わる人材の雇用が創出される可能性がある。 
最後は、技術・知識の波及である。データセンターを運営する外国企業が現地企業と取引すること
により、外国企業から現地企業へ技術が伝わることが期待される。例えば、電力・冷却・通信設備の
設計や施工、保守管理にかかるノウハウを、現地企業が獲得するケースなどがある。 
ただし、経済波及効果の実現には不確実性が伴う点は留意する必要がある。中でも、雇用への波及
効果は限定的なものに留まる可能性がある。というのも、fDi Marketsが集計する投資計画データによ
ると（図表6）、海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの直接雇用創出数は、データセンター投資の場合、全
産業平均よりも少数にとどまるからだ。

４．データセンター投資の先行きとリスク要因 
今後のデータセンター投資は、需要面での拡大余地が大きいと見込まれる一方、供給面では様々な
制約が表面化する可能性がある。 
まず、需要面については、クラウド需要の拡大がデータセンターの投資を促す公算が大きい。実際、
主要な民間調査会社の予想によれば、電子商取引やAI利活用の浸透によりデータの保存・処理需要が
増加し、アジアにおけるクラウド市場の拡大が続くとされている4。 
一方、先行きの供給制約となりうる要因も少なくない。具体的には、電力・水などの資源制約と、
政策変更の2つである。 
資源制約については、第一に電力供給の制約がある。アジアでは、今後も電力需要の拡大が見込ま
れる一方で、それに見合った電力供給の拡大が容易ではないからだ。図表7は、IEAが2026年2月に公表
した、2026～2030年の年平均増加率の予測を示している。掲載した各国・地域ではいずれも電力需要
の増加が見込まれ、特にインドやベトナムなどで高い伸びとなっていることがわかる。産業活動の拡
大や冷房需要の増加、データセンターの集積などが需要拡大をけん引する格好だ。 
一方、電力供給の伸びは、需要と比べ、緩やかなものにとどまる可能性が高い。IEAによれば、東南
アジアの電力生産量は2026～2030年に年平均5.2％の増加と、電力需要の伸び（年平均5.4％）を下回
り、電力需給のひっ迫が続くことが想定されている5。 
また、データセンターの立地が特定地域に集中しやすい点にも留意が必要だ6。特定地域で電力需給
がひっ迫した場合、送電網の不足などで他地域からの融通ができない場合がある7。 
資源制約については、電力のみならず、水の安定供給という課題もある。冷却に大量の水を使用す
るデータセンターでは、施設の新設・拡張に伴って水需要が増加すると見込まれるからだ。必要な水
量を確保できなければ、施設の稼働が制約されることになりかねない。ASEAN事務局（2025）は、デー
タセンターの冷却に伴う水需要の増加は、農業・工業・住民生活向けの水利用と競合する可能性があ
ると指摘している8。 
二点目は、政策変更のリスクである。雇用拡大等
を通じた地場経済への貢献度の低さや、資源利用や
安全性をめぐる地域住民からの反発などを背景に、
投資許認可の厳格化や新規開発の制限が行われる
可能性がある。 
地場経済への貢献については、先述の通り、デー
タセンターの直接的な雇用創出効果は他の投資に
比べて限られる。その結果、受入国・地域が期待し
ていた経済効果よりも現地経済への貢献が小さい
となった場合、投資誘致や優遇税制の見直しにつな
がる可能性は否定できない。また、地域住民からの
反発については、図表8で示す通り、既にマレーシ
ア、インドネシア、インド、オーストラリアなどで、
水利用や生活環境への影響などを巡り、データセン

ター投資への抗議や反対運動が起きている。 
こうした状況のもと、一部の国ではすでにデータセンター投資関連の規制を見直す動きが広がって
いる。実際にタイでは、政府による委員会組織（データセンタービジネス委員会）が、データセンタ
ー166件の建設や許可手続きを一時停止した。停止の理由としては、資源の利用、立地、安全性に関す
る全国的な基準の策定が必要であることが挙げられている9。タイ政府は投資誘致を維持すると示して
いるものの、予見可能性の低下から、進捗中案件の見直しや計画中案件の中止につながるリスクは完
全に拭えない。

５．供給制約への対応力がデータセンター投資の行方を左右 
アジアでは、AI・クラウド需要の拡大を背景に、今後もデータセンター投資の増加が続くと見込ま
れる。ただし、巨額の投資に比べて雇用創出効果は限定的とみられる一方、電力・水などのインフラ
負荷は大きく、投資の拡大が必ずしも受入国・地域に十分な経済的便益をもたらすとは限らない。 
こうした中、今後のデータセンター投資では、建設コストの低さや税制優遇に加え、電力・水の安
定供給や規制・許認可の予見可能性などが、投資先を選別する上で一段と重要になると考えられる。
供給制約が深刻化する地域では、投資計画の遅延や縮小が生じる一方、安定的な事業環境を確保でき
る地域に投資が集中する可能性もある。 
受入国にとっても、単に投資額の拡大を追求するのではなく、国内産業への波及やインフラ整備の
負担を見極めることが重要となる。今後のアジアにおけるデータセンター投資は、需要拡大が成長を
支える一方で、供給制約への対応力の違いが、各国・地域のへの投資配分を左右することになるだろ
う。

図表 8 データセンターを巡る抗議活動の例

延が減少し、データ送受信にかかる時間が短期化すると指摘されている。 
2 fDi Markets や生成 AI による情報収集により、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件に関する企業発表の内容を確認

付加価値の増加につながるわけではない。 
4 CBRE、McKinsey & Company 等の民間調査会社のレポートを参照した。 
5IEA（2026）“ASEAN Energy Security Review”では、東南アジアの電力需要は 2026～2030 年に年平均 5.4％増加すると予測さ

IEA の 2024 年実績値と 2025 年の前年比伸び率、2030 年の予測値を基準に、年平均成長率が一定となるよう補間し計算したも
の。 
6 IEA（2025）“Energy and AI”は、データセンターは電力負荷が大きく、地理的にも集中する傾向があるため、発電能力や送
電網容量を大幅に増強する必要が生じる可能性があること、また電力供給が途切れないよう安定的な電力網を構築する必要があ
ることを指摘している。 
7 ベトナム北部では、2023 年 5～6 月に断続的な停電が発生し、多くの家庭や企業に影響が広がった。World Bank（2023）
“Taking Stock, August 2023: Making Public Investment Work for Growth”は、送電網整備の遅れにより、南部の余剰発電

8 ASEAN（2025）“ASEAN Guide for Sustainable Data Centre Development”によれば、データセンターの中でもハイパースケ
ール施設の冷却システムでは、水使用量が 1 日当たり最大 150 万リットルに達する場合があり、干ばつが発生しやすい地域の水
供給や生態系に大きな負荷を与えうると説明している。また、水利用の効率化を促す政策的枠組みがなければ、データセンター
の水需要が、農業・工業・地域住民に必要な水の確保を圧迫する可能性を指摘している。 
9 「DC 開発 166 件を一時停止、全国的な基準策定へ」『NNA ASIA』、2026 年 9 月 7 日。

2026 年 11 月 9 日

レポート

2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

～企業の好業績と人手不足を背景に堅調な増加が続く～

調査部 主席研究員 小林 真一郎 
研究員 小林 啓介

○ 2026 年冬の民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）のボーナスは、前年比＋5.0%と 6 年連続で増
加が見込まれる。好調な企業業績と堅調な雇用情勢が追い風となり、大幅な伸びになるだろう。 
○ 支給労働者割合は 87.3%（前年差＋0.8%ポイント）と 2 年ぶりに上昇するだろう。同割合はコロナ前の水準
を超えて回復しており、雇用者数の増加が続く中、ボーナスが支給される事業所で働く労働者の数は 4,577
万人（前年比＋1.9%）と、4 年連続で過去最多を更新する見込みである。 
○ 一人当たり支給額と支給労働者数の増加を受け、ボーナスの支給総額は 20.4 兆円（前年比＋7.0%）と 6 年
連続で増加しよう。ボーナスの支給総額の伸びは物価上昇率を大きく上回っており、個人消費の回復を下
支えすると期待される。 
○ 2026 年冬の国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）のボーナス（期末・勤勉手当）の平均支
給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と前年冬に続き、大きめの増加が見込まれる。景気回復に伴う民間
給与の増加が公務員にも反映される状況が続き、基本給、ボーナス支給月数とも引き上げられるとみられる。 
2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

(注 1)民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）は、賞与を支給する事業所で働く全常用労働者（当該事業所で賞与
の支給を受けていない労働者も含む）の平均 
(注 2)国家公務員は、管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職の平均

1. 2026 年夏のボーナス ～企業の好業績を背景に 5 年連続の増加

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」によると、民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）における 2026
年夏のボーナスの一人当たり支給額は、前年比＋5.3%と 5 年連続で増加した（図表 1）。前年（2025 年）の
伸びである同＋2.9%を大きく上回っており、2026 年は企業の好業績を背景にボーナスは堅調に増加した。 
業種別では、製造業（前年比＋5.8%）、非製造業（同＋5.3%）ともに増加した。内訳をみると、生活関連サ
ービス等（前年比＋25.7%）、建設業（同＋14.4%）、学術研究等（同＋12.9%）などが大幅に増加し、全体
の数字を押し上げた。一方で、複合サービス事業（同－2.1%）は減少した。 
ボーナス支給月数（所定内給与に対する比率）は 1.10 カ月分と前年から 0.02 カ月増加した。また、ボー
ナスが支給された労働者の割合 1は 85.1%（前年差＋0.5%ポイント）と小幅に上昇した。雇用者数が順調に
増加して過去最高水準を更新しており、ボーナスが支給された労働者数は前年比＋1.4%と増加した。夏の
ボーナスの支給総額（平均給与額×支給労働者数）は 19.9 兆円（前年比＋6.8%）と過去最高を記録した。

（注 1）調査産業計、事業所規模 5 人以上  
（注 2）支給月数は所定内給与に対する支給割合 
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

める割合。

2. 2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

(1) 民間企業 ～ 企業の好業績と労働需給の逼迫を追い風に、6 年連続で増加する見込み

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」ベースで見た民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）の 2026 年冬
のボーナスの一人当たり平均支給額は 44 万 6,252 円（前年比＋5.0%）と 6 年連続での増加を予想する（図表
2）。企業規模を問わず、製造業、非製造業とも一人当たり平均支給額は増加すると予測する。 
今年の冬のボーナスが順調な増加を続けるとみられる背景に、企業業績の好調さと雇用情勢の堅調さがある。
企業の経常利益（全規模、金融保険業を除く全産業、季節調整値）は、2026 年 4～6 月期に過去最高を更新す
るなど好調に推移している。今後イラン情勢の展開次第では業績に下押し圧力がかかるリスクはあるが、冬のボ
ーナスへの影響は軽微だろう。 
また、人手不足は深刻化し、労働需給は逼迫している。日銀短観における雇用人員判断 DI は対面型サービ
ス業を中心に大幅な「不足」超が続き、企業の人手不足感が強まる中、完全失業率は 2％台半ばと低水準での推
移が続いている。

図表 2 冬のボーナス予測：平均支給額（前年比）と支給月数

（注 1）調査産業計、事業所規模 5 人以上  
（注 2）支給月数は所定内給与に対する支給割合 
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

支給総額の伸びは物価上昇率を大きく上回っており、ボーナス支給総額の増加が個人消費の回復を下支えす
る要因になると期待される。

(2) 公務員 ～ 基本給、支給月数ともプラスに寄与し、ボーナスは 5 年連続で増加

内閣人事局の発表によると、国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）の 2026 年夏のボーナス
（期末・勤勉手当）は 73 万 8,500 円（前年比＋4.5%）と 4 年連続で増加した。昨年の人事院勧告に基づく給与
法改正でボーナスの基準となる基本給が前年比＋3.3%と増加したほか、ボーナス支給月数が昨夏から 0.025 カ
月引き上げられた。 
2026 年冬のボーナスの平均支給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と、5 年連続で増加すると予測する（図
表 8）。今年の人事院勧告に沿った給与法改正が行われることを前提に、平均給与額が増加（＋3.3%）するととも
に、ボーナス支給月数が昨冬から 0.025 カ月分（勤勉手当、期末手当がそれぞれ 0.0125 カ月分）引き上げられ
ると想定する。支給額は夏冬通じて過去最高を更新し、景気回復に伴う民間給与の増加が公務員にも反映され
る状況が続くとみられる。今後も、民間の賃金上昇傾向が続く中、公務員のボーナスも高めの伸びが続く見込み
である。

Zhen Ding Technology (4958.TW)

4
Zhen Ding Technology
Valuation
We set a DCF-based TP of NT$750. We use a DCF-based valuation to reflect ZDT's ability to generate stable cash flows. Key
assumptions of our DCF model include a risk-free rate of 5.2% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk premium of 7%,
and an equity beta of 0.89 (1-year weekly share price), deriving a WACC of 10.7%. Our model assumes high-single-digit long-

term growth after 2028E and a 21.6% EBIT margin from 2028E with a gradual annual increase. Our TP is equivalent to 27x/17x
2027E/28E EPS and 4.5x/3.8x 2027E/28E BVPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause the stock to trade below our target price include: 1) lower-than-expected
iPhone/Mac/iPad end-demand; 2) lower content value for new smartphone models or pricing pressure; 3) ZDT losing market
share from its FPC/HDI peers; 4) a slower demand in the substrate industry; 5) failure to pass the qualifications of major AI

GPU or ASIC customers.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo, we lower our forecasts and 
TP for M31, as we factor in its still suboptimal scale and delayed 
employment bonus in 3Q26 that weigh on its trajectory of a bottom-line 
turnaround. That said, we remain comfortable with our Buy (1) call on the 
stock as we expect M31’s turnaround, kicking off from 3Q26 despite at a 
small scale, to sustain into 2027 with meaningful incremental builds, 
supported by its conversion from license to royalty revenue paying off, and 
broadened footprint into advanced technologies for price accretion and into 
diversified foundry, IDM, and fabless customers for volume accretion.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes. We lower our 2026-27E EPS for 
M31 by 14-43% to embed its still suboptimal scale and opex overhang in 
our model. Despite the cuts, its bottom-line turnaround met our expectation, 
and we expect the turnaround to sustain with meaningful incremental builds 
sequentially through 2027 to improve market confidence. We forecast its 
4Q26 top line to rebound by 23% QoQ (+12% YoY) for EPS to reach 
TWD3.31 (TWD0.35 in 3Q26), setting a good start for M31’s 2027 outlook 
and warranting a strong 213% EPS CAGR over 2026-28E.

Turnaround confirmed. While prolonged license project recognitions 
delayed M31’s turnaround, depicted in our previous Flash, we believe its 
bumpy operations over the past 2 years is finally coming to an end, 
evidenced by management’s comments of QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26 
with EPS expanding to cover accumulated losses YTD. M31 has actively 
invested over the past years enriching its IP portfolio, accumulating more 
than 2,000 license projects since 2012 (chart on p.2), and setting a good 
foundation for converting them to royalty revenue. It aims for the royalty 
revenue to outgrow license revenue in the next few quarters, after years of 
“cooking”. Meanwhile, two structural growth drivers remain intact for ASP 
and volume accretion: 1) for ASP, a growing number of high-value projects 
from advanced process nodes to enhance M31’s IP portfolio for ASP 
accretion, and 2) for volume, an increased diversification of customer base 
globally to broaden its addressable markets for volume accretion.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we lower our 12M TP 
for M31 to TWD550 (previous: TWD645), still based on our 4-quarter 
forward PER of 45x. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with key downside risks 
of silicon inflation and geopolitical overhang leading to global inflation and 
AI capex cut to delay M31’s royalty ramps.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 52% above the consensus, likely due to 
our more positive view on royalty revenue ramp.

10 November 2026

Structural turnaround round the corner (III)

➢ Lowering our forecasts to reflect still suboptimal scale… 
➢ … but expecting enriched license projects to bear fruit soon 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating with a lower 12M TP of TWD550

Company profile 
Founded in 2011, M31 is an independent semiconductor IP provider in Taiwan, offering both foundation 
IP for specialty technology nodes and functional IP such as high-speed interface and analogue IP.

### Page 5

5

M31 Technology (6643 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 11 Mar 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
M31's board of directors (BoD) has 7 members, including 3 independent directors, who
represent 43% of total directors, higher than the regulatory requirement of at least 20%. Its
Chairman and President are not the same person, but its President is one of the directors. M31
has the Audit Committee, Compensation Committee and Risk Control Committee under the
BoD.
Capital management 1
Although M31 is a young company (founded in 2011 and listed in 2019), its dividend payout ratio
has been over the 75% level for the past 5 years. M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without
onsite production activities, which would require high capex to do so. As such, we view its high
dividend payout ratio to be appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 We do not see any meaningful impact from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
M31's suppliers are required to comply with the Supplier Management Standards, which was set
by M31 in 2013. In addition, M31 has periodical evaluations for its suppliers and onsite audits to
ensure the quality and implementation of Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR), as well as
Environmental Protection and Safety and Integrity Policies.
E Water & wastewater management 1 M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production activity to affect the environment.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
The company is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production
activity to affect the environment. It still adopts some measures to reduce waste from its
operations, such as recycling and using electronic files to replace paper documents as much as
possible to reduce the use of paper.
E GHG emissions 1 M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production activity to affect the environment.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

투자의견 BUY

목표주가 500,000

현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

• The Cotality home value index – covering
all of Australia – fell 1.2% in October
following a 1.3% decline in September
and 1.4% falls in August and July.

Annual growth has turned negative,
running at –2.8%yr with prices down
6.7% from their peak in March.
• Across the major cities, Brisbane and
Perth led the declines in the October
month, recording falls of 2.0% and
1.7% respectively, but the correction
remains more pronounced in Sydney
(prices down a further 1.6%mth to be
off 9.4%yr) with Melbourne also off
more sharply over the year (–7.6%yr)
but recording a milder October
(–0.8%mth). Adelaide remains relatively

resilient, recording a 1.2% decline in
the month but prices still up 3.7%yr.
• Preliminary estimates show the
number of sales, in rolling 3mth
terms, down 5.6%qtr and 22.5%yr.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia, fell
1.2% in October with slight revisions to previous months
again shaving a further 0.3% off prices. The correction
has now seen prices decline 6.7% from their March peak

with annual growth firmly in negative at –2.8%yr. Turnover
continues fall as well, down 22.5%yr. Housing-related
tax policy changes, interest rate rises and heightened
uncertainty have triggered a sharp contraction in markets.
The downturn is now comparable to the previous two
housing market corrections. Nationally, the downturn in
2022-23 saw prices decline 7.5% and turnover fall 29% peak
to trough. The 2017-18 correction saw prices fall 8.6% and

turnover by 24%, with larger falls in Sydney and Melbourne.
Note that unless specified, figures in titles, tables and
commentary are in non‑seasonally adjusted terms, consistent
with the ‘headline’ figures reported by Cotality. However,
figures shown in charts are in seasonally adjusted terms.
Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 5.6%qtr in October, previous estimates again revised
lower. The monthly pattern suggests declines re‑accelerated

through September‑October after stabilising somewhat in July‑
August. The RBA's interest rate rise in late September and a
further expected tightening likely contributed to the shift.
The detail shows Brisbane and Perth are now leading the
way in terms of monthly price declines although corrections
to date remain significantly larger in Sydney and Melbourne
while smaller capitals and regional areas continue to see much
milder downturns. Across price tiers, all segments are now

showing similar rates of decline after top price tiers had seen
a more pronounced and earlier downturn. 'Top tier' segments
have tended to lead recent price cycles.
Looking across jurisdictions,
• Sydney prices dropped 1.6% in October having averaged
similar‑sized declines over the previous five months. While
all segments and tiers continue to record falls, the last few
months has seen the pace of decline moderate a touch in the
top tier segments that have led cycles in the past, although

they are still a long way from stabilisation.
• Melbourne prices declined 0.8%mth, in line with
September's move and continuing the slower pace of
retreat. As noted, this likely reflects the weaker starting
point coming into the correction, prices already at 5½ year
lows. Interestingly, the detail shows the moderation in price
declines in recent months has been more pronounced for
'top tier' units where prices are back where they were in
2016.
Housing downturn deepens

3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
• Brisbane prices are tracking a very fast rate of decline
that is also seeing large downward revisions. Prices fall
2% in October with September and August now both
showing 2.1% falls, revised down from 1.5% and 1.6%

respectively (August having previously been estimated at
just 1%, July's move having seen a similar series of revisions
–1.2% to –1.8%). The combined effect has taken the rolling
3mth change to –6.1%qtr from –4.7%qtr last months and
–2.7%qtr in August and –0.6%qtr in July. At 0.4%yr, annual
price growth has virtually stalled flat having run at 18%yr as
recently as May. All tiers and segments are recording similar
rates of decline.
• Perth is seeing something very similar, prices dropping a

further 1.7% in October, with September's decline revised
down to 2.1% from –1.2% and August's to 2.0% from –1.7%.
At 3.9%yr, annual growth is still positive but off steeply
from the 24% pace back in May. As with Brisbane, all tiers
and segments are recording similar rates of decline.
• Adelaide dwelling prices posted a somewhat milder 1.2%
decline in October, , September's –1.3% decline marked
down to –1.7%. Annual price growth slowed to 3.7%yr,

similar to Perth's but with less of a whiplash from strong
gains to large declines. Turnover has also been much
steadier, sales holding flat over the last three months and
down just 12.4%yr.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.6%mth, 9.7%yr), relatively resilient
in Hobart (+0.5%mth, 5.5%yr) but weaker in Canberra
(–0.7%mth, –3.7%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.6%). Annual

prices growth is in the 1.4‑3.5%yr range.
All up, the October updates make for bleak reading. With the
RBA widely expected to raise interest rates again, the near
term outlook remains very challenging. Next week's consumer
sentiment update will show how this is shaping housing‑
related sentiment – so far, consumer price expectations have
weakened but stopped short of swinging to the clear negative
consensus that emerged in the 2022‑23 correction. That now

looks to be at risk. Either way, momentum looks set to remain
weak into year‑end and, barring a significantly improved
inflation and interest rate outlook, well into 2027.

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.”2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.

complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings

(real final sales to private domestic

ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Investment Implications
Foreign investors remain keen on

is bullish for U.S. securities, both near-

term and long-term.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.

of Bank of America Corporation.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Document 346: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-17)

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
FINANCE BULLETIN
Steep fall
11 November 2026
Key points
• The value of new dwelling finance
approvals excl. refinance dropped
15.5%qtr, the biggest quarterly decline
on records going back to 1991. Annual
growth swung to –18.1%yr. While the

decline was widely expected, it was
larger than the median forecast of -8.5%
and Westpac’s forecast of –10%.
• The quarterly drop was mostly driven
by lower volumes: total loan numbers
down 15%qtr (-21%yr). Smaller average
loan size contributed a further 0.5ppts
to the value decline in the quarter.
• Investors were the primary driver,
volumes in this segment down
over 30%qtr and the value of new
loans off by nearly a third.
• Borrowing by owner occupiers

significant but somewhat milder
declines, both the volume and value
of new loans down 5.3%qtr.
• More weakness looks likely for Q4.
Q3 new dwelling
finance:
–15.5%qtr,
–18.1%yr
New finance approvals by segment: value

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The value of new dwelling finance approvals fell steeply
in Q3, a 15%qtr drop to be down 25% year to date. The
quarterly fall was the largest since the series began in
1991 while the fall over the last nine months is on a par
with the 2022 downturn, which was the largest on record.

The outcome was significantly weaker than the consensus
forecast of an 8.5% drop. The downturn reflects the
combined impact of rising interest rates and major housing-
related tax policy changes announced at the May budget
which have hit investor loans for the purchase of existing
dwellings particularly hard.
The detail shows volumes were the main driver, the number
of new loans dropping 15%qtr with the average loan size
accounting for 0.5ppts of the quarterly decline in the total

value of new loans.
The segment split showed a 5.3%qtr fall in new owner
occupier loan approvals and a whopping 30% drop in new
investor loans. Within the latter, the number of investor
loans for the purchase of existing dwellings (which no longer
receive ‘negative gearing’ and capital gains tax concessions)
fell 35.6%qtr and are down by a half over the year to date. In
contrast, the number of investor loans for the construction or
purchase of newly built dwellings (which are still eligible for

concessions) declined 8.7%qtr and 8.5% year-to-date. This
segment now accounts for 27.6% of all investor loans, up from
17% late last year.
Owner-occupier loans reflected the wider influence of interest
rate and market uncertainty. The detail here shows a more
pronounced 8.6%qtr drop in first home buyer numbers, a more
interest rate sensitive segment. High construction costs also
look to be a factor with the number of owner occupier loans for
the construction and purchase of newly built dwellings down

11.9%qtr. Lending for land purchases and renovations also
declined 12.9%qtr in value terms.
Today’s results were weaker than expected with a bigger
pull-back in investor activity than we assessed when the tax
policy changes were announced back in May. Some of this may
be an overshoot with uncertainty about policy changes and
the wider price correction discouraging investors who might
otherwise have considered purchasing even without the tax
benefits. That said, conditions look far from conducive for even

a muted bounce near term. More weak reads look likely for the
final quarter of 2026.
Bigger than expected investor pull back

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
All major central banks are now in a rate-hiking phase. The
stance of policy nonetheless varies considerably across
economies, as does the policy outlook.
The Bank of England and Bank of Canada increased their
policy rates for the first time this cycle and the RBA a fifth time

since the last Market Outlook. The pace of increase is generally
moderate: the FOMC and RBNZ paused at their latest
meetings – the RBNZ unexpectedly so – but both are expected
to lift policy rates further. Policy is likely still accommodative
in New Zealand and Canada, consistent with the economic and
labour market slack evident in these economies. In Australia it
is clearly restrictive, as discussed below.
The US is contending with sticky inflation, as evidenced by

recent CPI and labour market data. We have long been of the
view that the US is likely to be structurally more inflationary
than many peers, and concerns about inflation are increasingly
coming to the fore in FOMC members’ comments. Accordingly
we have added another hike in January to our US rates view, on
top of the one in December already forecast (see page 20).
The main, but not sole, driver of the pivot to tightening has been
the energy price shock. That said, exclusion-based core inflation

measures (excluding food and energy) are also above central
bank targets. Market pricing of future central bank moves had
been sensitive to oil prices and the outlook in the Middle East
earlier in the year. That correlation broke down during the bond
sell-off, which was mostly a phenomenon of real yields, but
seems to have  re-emerged in the pricing for policy in 2027.
Spot and daated Brent oil prices have fallen markedly in recent
days as tensions have eased and negotiations appear to have

restarted. This has not affected very near-term pricing of central
bank decisions, and an initial moderation of mid-2027 pricing
retraced after the strong US CPI print and some Fed speeches.
Further along the yield curve, last month’s sell-off in bond
markets has largely reversed. Unlike the increase in yields,
when longer-run market-based measures of inflation
expectations relatively stable, the more recent rally has
involved some decline in these expectations measures. Most of

the adjustment has been front-loaded: the 1-year expectations
derived from swaps have declined 45bps for Australia and
nearly 60bps for the US since the beginning of November.
However, inflation expectations derived for future years have
also moderated. The 5-year-5-year forward measures derived
from swaps and breakevens have both declined, with the latter
noticeably below the 2.5% RBA target midpoint. The same
measures for the US have also declined over this period, but by

less, and remain further above the Fed’s 2% target, consistent
with our view about inflation pressures there.
Global asset market rotation ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
